'''Max Emilian Verstappen''' ({{IPA|nl|ˈmɑks fɛrˈstɑpə(n)}}; born 30 September 1997) is a Dutch and Belgian [[racing driver]] who competes [[Formula One drivers from the Netherlands|under the Dutch flag]] in [[Formula One]] for [[Red Bull Racing]]. Verstappen has won four [[Formula One World Drivers' Championship titles]], which he won consecutively from {{F1|2021}} to {{F1|2024}} with Red Bull, and has won {{f1stat|VER|wins}} [[Formula One Grands Prix|Grands Prix]] across 11 seasons.

Born in [[Hasselt]] and raised in [[Maaseik]], Verstappen is the son of Dutch former Formula One driver [[Jos Verstappen]] and Belgian former [[kart racer]] [[Sophie Kumpen]]. After a successful karting career—culminating in his [[#2013: Record-breaking season|record-breaking 2013 season]]—Verstappen graduated to [[junior formulae]]. Progressing directly to [[FIA Formula 3 European Championship|FIA European Formula 3]], Verstappen broke several records on his way to third in the championship in [[2014 FIA Formula 3 European Championship|his rookie season]] with [[Van Amersfoort]].{{efn|Including most consecutive wins (6), youngest winner (16 years, 216 days), and youngest polesitter (16 years, 215 days).}} Aged 17, Verstappen signed for [[Toro Rosso]] in {{F1|2015}} as part of the [[Red Bull Junior Team]], becoming the [[List of Formula One driver records#Youngest drivers to start a race|youngest driver in Formula One history]] at the {{F1GP|2015|Australian}}. Following several points finishes in his debut season, Verstappen retained his seat for {{F1|2016}} before being promoted to parent team [[Red Bull Racing|Red Bull]] after four rounds. On debut for Red Bull, aged 18, Verstappen won the {{F1GP|2016|Spanish}}, becoming the [[List of Formula One driver records#Youngest winners|youngest-ever driver to win a Formula One Grand Prix]]. Verstappen achieved multiple race wins in his {{F1|2017}} and {{F1|2018}} campaigns, before finishing third in both the {{F1|2019}} and {{F1|2020}} World Drivers' Championships [[Honda in Formula One|under Honda power]].

Verstappen won his maiden title in {{F1|2021}} after overtaking [[Lewis Hamilton]] on the final lap of the [[2021 Abu Dhabi Grand Prix|last race of the season]], becoming the [[Formula One drivers from the Netherlands|first World Drivers' Champion from the Netherlands]]. Verstappen won the next two championships in {{F1|2022}} and {{F1|2023}}, overturning the largest points deficit in Formula One history in the former and breaking numerous records across both seasons.{{efn|See [[#Formula One records|§ Formula One records]].}} He secured his fourth consecutive title in {{F1|2024}} after winning nine Grands Prix, including a widely acclaimed wet-weather performance in [[2024 São Paulo Grand Prix|São Paulo]], to become the first driver to win the championship driving for a third-placed constructor in [[1983 Formula One World Championship|41 years]]. He finished runner-up by two points in a three-way title battle with [[Lando Norris]] and [[Oscar Piastri]] in {{F1|2025}}.

As of the {{Latest F1GP}}, Verstappen has achieved {{f1stat|VER|wins}} race wins, {{f1stat|VER|poles}} pole positions, {{f1stat|VER|fastestlaps}} fastest laps, and {{f1stat|VER|podiums}} podiums in Formula One. In addition to being the youngest Grand Prix winner, he holds several [[List of Formula One driver records|Formula One records]], including the [[List of Formula One driver records#Most wins in a season|most wins in a season]] (19), the [[List of Formula One driver records#Most podium finishes in a season|most podium finishes in a season]] (21), the [[List of Formula One driver records#Most consecutive wins|most consecutive wins]] (10), and the [[List of Formula One driver records#Most consecutive pole positions|most consecutive pole positions]] (8, shared with [[Ayrton Senna]]). Verstappen is contracted to remain at Red Bull until at least the end of the 2028 season.<ref name=":3">{{cite web |date=3 March 2022 |title=Max Verstappen signs bumper new deal with Red Bull until end of 2028 |url=https://www.formula1.com/en/latest/article.breaking-verstappen-signs-bumper-new-deal-with-red-bull-until-end-of-2028.7Csixu7ehVLNr5mDgFQ1Q7.html |access-date=3 March 2022 |publisher=Formula One |archive-date=3 March 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220303100618/https://www.formula1.com/en/latest/article.breaking-verstappen-signs-bumper-new-deal-with-red-bull-until-end-of-2028.7Csixu7ehVLNr5mDgFQ1Q7.html |url-status=live}}</ref> He has also competed professionally in [[sim racing]] since 2015, winning several marquee ''[[iRacing]]'' events. Verstappen was listed in the 2024 issue of ''[[Time (magazine)|Time]]'' as one of the [[Time 100|100 most influential people globally]], and was appointed an [[Officer of the Order of Orange-Nassau]] in 2022.

==Early life==
Max Emilian Verstappen was born on 30 September 1997 in [[Hasselt]], Belgium to [[Jos Verstappen]] and [[Sophie Kumpen]].<ref>{{cite web |url=http://en.espnf1.com/f1/motorsport/driver/171391.html |title=Drivers / Max Verstappen |publisher=ESPN F1 |access-date=20 August 2014|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150321042436/http://en.espnf1.com/f1/motorsport/driver/171391.html|archive-date=21 March 2015}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|title=Max Verstappen with his sister Victoria Jane Verstappen and his father Jos Verstappen|url=http://en.espnf1.com/f1/motorsport/image/217737.html|access-date=14 December 2020|publisher=ESPN UK|archive-date=13 October 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20221013203349/https://www.espn.com/f1/|url-status=live}}</ref> Verstappen's parents separated when he was young, after which he lived with his father. His younger sister, Victoria, lived with their mother.<ref>{{Cite web|date=10 December 2021|title=How Max Verstappen's hard-nosed upbringing created a driver ready for war with Lewis Hamilton|url=https://inews.co.uk/sport/formula-one/max-verstappen-lewis-hamilton-dangerous-driving-1341419|access-date=21 December 2021|website=inews.co.uk|archive-date=21 December 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211221184551/https://inews.co.uk/sport/formula-one/max-verstappen-lewis-hamilton-dangerous-driving-1341419|url-status=live}}</ref> Verstappen has three younger half-siblings from his father: a sister from Jos's second marriage and a brother and sister from Jos's current marriage.<ref name=":2">{{Cite web|title=Jos Verstappen becomes a father for a fourth time|url=https://www.gptoday.net/en/news/f1/247258/jos-verstappen-becomes-a-father-for-a-fourth-time|access-date=21 December 2021|website=GPToday.net|date=2 May 2019 |archive-date=21 December 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211221184601/https://www.gptoday.net/en/news/f1/247258/jos-verstappen-becomes-a-father-for-a-fourth-time|url-status=live}}</ref>

His family has a long association with motor sports: his father is a Dutch former Formula One driver, his Belgian mother competed in [[Kart racing|karting]],<ref name="racing family2">{{cite web|last1=Bonardel|first1=Cécile|date=18 August 2014|title=Verstappen's son in F1 in 2015|url=http://www.24h-lemans.com/en/news/24-hours-of-le-mans-previous-class-winner-jos-verstappen-s-son-in-f1-in-2015_2_2_1746_16741.html|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20141129073628/http://www.24h-lemans.com/en/news/24-hours-of-le-mans-previous-class-winner-jos-verstappen-s-son-in-f1-in-2015_2_2_1746_16741.html|archive-date=29 November 2014|access-date=28 May 2019|website=24h-lemans.com}}</ref><ref name="nationality2">{{cite news|last=Cornelissen|first=Marc|date=17 August 2014|title=Geen rijbewijs, wel in de F1|language=nl|trans-title=No driver's licence, yet in F1|work=Nieuwsblad.be|url=http://www.nieuwsblad.be/sportwereld/cnt/dmf20140816_01219423|access-date=12 December 2014|quote=Max heeft een Belgische moeder, is geboren in Hasselt, woont in Bree en reist met een Belgisch paspoort. Genetisch en cultureel is hij Belgischer dan de gemiddelde Rode Duivel. Maar racen doet hij met een Nederlandse licentie. 'Ik heb heel mijn leven in België gewoond, maar ik voel meer Nederlander. Door al dat karten trek ik meer met mijn vader op dan met mijn moeder. Ik zit gewoon altijd tussen de Nederlanders. [Max has a Belgian mother, was born in Hasselt, lives in Bree, and travels with a Belgian passport. From a genetic and cultural perspective, he's more Belgian than the average Red Devil. Yet he competes with a Dutch racing licence. 'I have lived in Belgium my entire life, but I consider myself more Dutch. As a result of my karting activities I spend more time with my father than with my mother. I'm always surrounded by Dutch people.']|archive-date=8 March 2015|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150308132510/http://www.nieuwsblad.be/sportwereld/cnt/dmf20140816_01219423|url-status=live}}</ref> and his first cousin once removed, [[Anthony Kumpen]], competed in endurance racing and is a two-time [[NASCAR Whelen Euro Series]] champion currently serving as the team manager for [[PK Carsport]] in Euro Series.<ref name="racing family2" /> Verstappen initially attended a secondary school in [[Maaseik]], before moving into [[private tutor]]ing; he admitted that he used to [[truancy|leave class early]] to attend races across Europe with his father.<ref>{{Cite web |url=https://f1i.com/news/371167-clever-verstappen-reveals-ploy-to-avoid-school-and-go-racing.html |title=Clever Verstappen remembers ploy to avoid school and go racing |last=van Osten |first=Phillip |date=2 April 2020 |website=F1i.com |access-date=6 December 2024 |archive-date=8 December 2024 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20241208005612/https://f1i.com/news/371167-clever-verstappen-reveals-ploy-to-avoid-school-and-go-racing.html |url-status=live }}</ref>

==Junior racing career==
===Karting (2005–2013)===
====2005–2009: Dominance in national championships====
Verstappen started racing in karts at the age of four and competing in championships at the age of seven. In 2005 he competed in the Mini Junior championship of his home province of [[Limburg (Belgium)|Limburg]], Belgium<ref>{{cite web|title=Limburgs Kart Championship&nbsp;– Mini-Junior 2005|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/limburgs-kart-championship---mini-junior/2005/|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=27 January 2014|archive-date=22 May 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210522154144/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/limburgs-kart-championship---mini-junior/2005/|url-status=live}}</ref> and won the [[Flanders|VAS]] Championship in the same category. In 2006, he repeated his success. In 2007, Verstappen graduated to the [[Rotax Max]] Mini Max class and won the Rotax Max Challenge Belgium<ref>{{cite web|title=Rotax Max Challenge Belgium Minimax 2007|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/rotax-max-challenge-belgium-minimax/2007/|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=27 January 2014|archive-date=24 August 2014|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140824014222/http://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/rotax-max-challenge-belgium-minimax/2007/|url-status=live}}</ref> and the Dutch Championship<ref>{{cite web|title=Dutch Championship Minimax 2007|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/dutch-championship-rotax-max-minimax/2007/|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=15 October 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230531002332/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/dutch-championship-rotax-max-minimax/2007/|archive-date=31 May 2023|url-status=live}}</ref> in that category. In 2008, racing in a [[CRG (kart manufacturer)|CRG]] kart entered by his father, Verstappen won the Belgian Cadet Championship,<ref>{{cite web|title=Belgian Championship&nbsp;– Cadet 2008|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/belgian-championship---cadet/2008/|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=27 January 2014|archive-date=22 May 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210522154236/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/belgian-championship---cadet/2008/|url-status=live}}</ref> the Rotax Max Challenge Belgium,<ref>{{cite web|title=Rotax Max Challenge Belgium Minimax 2008|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/rotax-max-challenge-belgium-minimax/2008/|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=27 January 2014|archive-date=22 May 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210522154239/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/rotax-max-challenge-belgium-minimax/2008/|url-status=live}}</ref> and the Benelux Karting Series in Mini Max class.<ref>{{cite web|title=BNL Karting Series Minimax 2008|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/bnl-karting-series---minimax/2008/|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=15 October 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230531003941/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/bnl-karting-series---minimax/2008/|archive-date=31 May 2023|url-status=live}}</ref> In 2009, Verstappen joined Pex Racing, a CRG customer team. That year, he defended his titles in the Mini Max championships<ref>{{cite web|title=VAS Championship&nbsp;– Rotax Max Challenge Belgium Minimax 2009|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/vas-championship---rotax-minimax/2009/|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=27 January 2014|archive-date=22 May 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210522154235/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/vas-championship---rotax-minimax/2009/|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{cite web|title=BNL Karting Series Mini Max 2009|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/bnl-karting-series---minimax/2009|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=15 October 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230530235643/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/bnl-karting-series---minimax/2009|archive-date=30 May 2023|url-status=live}}</ref> and won the Belgian KF5 Championship.<ref>{{cite web|title=Belgian Championship&nbsp;– KF5 2009|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/belgian-championship---kf5/2009/|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=27 January 2014|archive-date=22 May 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210522154217/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/belgian-championship---kf5/2009/|url-status=live}}</ref>

====2010–2012: Entering the international karting scene====
Verstappen was signed by CRG to race in their factory team and stepped up to international karting in 2010. At the [[KF3]] [[FIA Karting World Championship|World Cup]], Verstappen finished second to the more experienced [[Alexander Albon]] (who eventually became his Formula One teammate at Red Bull Racing)<ref>{{cite web|title=CIK-FIA World Cup&nbsp;– KF3 2010|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/world-cup---kf3/2010/|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=27 January 2014|archive-date=22 May 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210522154227/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/world-cup---kf3/2010/|url-status=live}}</ref> but beat him at the [[2010 WSK Euro Series|WSK Euro Series]]<ref>{{cite web|title=WSK Euro Series – KF3 2010|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/wsk-euro-series---kf3/2010|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=15 October 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230531001112/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/wsk-euro-series---kf3/2010|archive-date=31 May 2023|url-status=live}}</ref>  and also won the WSK World Series, beating [[Robert Vișoiu]].<ref>{{cite web|title=WSK World Series&nbsp;– KF3 2010|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/wsk-world-series---kf3/2010/|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=27 January 2014|archive-date=30 July 2014|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140730035255/http://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/wsk-world-series---kf3/2010/|url-status=live}}</ref> He finished fifth in the [[CIK-FIA Karting European Championship|European Championship]] and won the WSK Nations Cup<ref>{{cite web|title=WSK Nations Cup KF3 2010|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/wsk-nations-cup---kf3/2010|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=15 October 2023|archive-date=31 May 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230531003715/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/wsk-nations-cup---kf3/2010|url-status=live}}</ref> as well as the final of the Bridgestone Cup where during a 28 laps' race in the rain he beat [[Dennis Olsen (racing driver)|Dennis Olsen]] by over 11 seconds.<ref>{{cite web|title=Bridgestone Cup Final KF3 2010|url=https://news.verstappen.com/pdf/2010/2010-10-24_Bridgestone-Cup_Final_KF3.pdf|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231010192601/https://news.verstappen.com/pdf/2010/2010-10-24_Bridgestone-Cup_Final_KF3.pdf|archive-date=10 October 2023|url-status=live|access-date=16 October 2023}}</ref> In 2011, Verstappen won the [[2011 WSK Euro Series|WSK Euro Series]] ahead of [[Esteban Ocon]] in a [[Italian American Motor Engineering|Parilla]]-powered CRG kart.<ref>{{cite web|title=WSK Euro Series&nbsp;– KF3 2011|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/wsk-euro-series---kf3/2011/|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=27 January 2014|archive-date=27 April 2014|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140427010816/http://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/wsk-euro-series---kf3/2011/|url-status=live}}</ref> However, it was not a successful year for him, as he finished 14th in the [[CIK-FIA Karting European Championship|European Championship]] and got taken out in the [[FIA Karting World Championship|World Cup]] by [[Charles Leclerc]].

In the following year, Verstappen joined the Intrepid Driver Program to race in the [[KF2]] and [[KZ2]] classes. He won the [[WSK Super Master Series|WSK Master Series]] in the KF2 class, beating the CRG driver Felice Tiene.<ref>{{cite web|title=WSK Master Series&nbsp;– KF2 2012|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/wsk-master-series---kf2/2012/|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=27 January 2014|archive-date=22 May 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210522154309/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/wsk-master-series---kf2/2012/|url-status=live}}</ref> Verstappen won the South Garda Winter Cup in the KF2 class, beating [[Dennis Olsen (racing driver)|Dennis Olsen]] and [[Antonio Fuoco]].<ref>{{cite web|title=17° Winter Cup&nbsp;– KF2 2012|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/south-garda-winter-cup---kf2/2012/|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=27 January 2014|archive-date=22 May 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210522154314/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/south-garda-winter-cup---kf2/2012/|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{cite web|title=SKUSA SuperNationals XVI&nbsp;– KZ2 2012|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/skusa-supernationals---kz2/2012/|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=27 January 2014|archive-date=22 May 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210522154319/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/standings/skusa-supernationals---kz2/2012/|url-status=live}}</ref> He finished sixth in the [[WSK Euro Series]].<ref>{{cite web|title=WSK Euro Series – KF2 2012|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/wsk-euro-series---kf2/2012|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=15 October 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230605105850/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/wsk-euro-series---kf2/2012|archive-date=5 June 2023|url-status=live}}</ref> He was the youngest driver taking part in the World Championship in [[KF1]] class and finished eight.<ref>{{cite web|title=World Championship– KF1 2012|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/world-championship-kf1/2012|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=15 October 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230531002806/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/world-championship-kf1/2012|archive-date=31 May 2023|url-status=live}}</ref>

In July 2012, it was announced that Verstappen would leave Intrepid. After a short stint with CRG-built [[Alex Zanardi|Zanardi]] karts, Verstappen returned to the factory CRG team.<ref>{{cite web|title=Max rijdt rest van seizoen op CRG kart met TM motor|url=http://www.verstappen.nl/?locatie=bericht&id=8038|work=Verstappen Info Page|access-date=27 January 2014|archive-date=5 February 2015|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150205012637/http://www.verstappen.nl/?locatie=bericht&id=8038|url-status=live}}</ref> He finished second at the World Cup in the KF2 class<ref>{{cite web|title=World Cup– KF2 2012|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/world-cup---kf2/2012|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=15 October 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230605092022/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/world-cup---kf2/2012|archive-date=5 June 2023|url-status=live}}</ref> and competed at the SKUSA SuperNationals in the KZ2 class in a CRG, finishing 21st.

====2013: Record-breaking season====
In 2013, Verstappen completed a record-breaking season in karting, having progressed to the senior [[KF2|KF]] and [[KZ (karting)|KZ]] categories. Aged 15, he won three [[CIK-FIA]] championships in a single season: two [[Karting European Championship|European Championship]]s and a [[Karting World Championship|World Championship]]; an unprecedented feat in the history of the discipline.<ref>{{Cite web|title=Verstappen sets Karting world record|url=https://www.fiakarting.com/news/max-verstappen-an-exceptional-season-with-more-th|website=fiakarting.com|access-date=15 February 2022|archive-date=15 February 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220215021715/https://www.fiakarting.com/news/max-verstappen-an-exceptional-season-with-more-th|url-status=live}}</ref> He became the first driver since his father [[Jos Verstappen|Jos]] to win two European Championships in the same season, and the first to do so in both the primary [[direct-drive]] and [[gearbox]] classes. By winning the 2013 [[Karting World Championship#125 Gearbox FIA Karting world champions and World Cup winners|KZ World Championship]] at [[Varennes-sur-Allier]], beating [[Charles Leclerc]] to the title, Verstappen became the youngest-ever driver to win the gearbox World Championship.<ref>{{Cite web|date=24 February 2014|title=Verstappen is KZ World Champion at 15!|url=https://www.fia.com/news/verstappen-kz-world-champion-15|access-date=20 January 2022|website=[[Fédération Internationale de l'Automobile]]|archive-date=20 January 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220120232052/https://www.fia.com/news/verstappen-kz-world-champion-15|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{cite web|title=World Championship – KZ 2013|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/world-championship-kz/2013|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=9 October 2023|archive-date=10 October 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231010192606/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/world-championship-kz/2013|url-status=live}}</ref>

Further adding to his accolades in 2013, Verstappen also finished third at the [[Karting World Championship#FIA Karting World champions (direct drive & primary category)|KF World Championship]] after being disqualified for a move on [[Nicklas Nielsen]] in the final race, having won the opening round and pre-final.<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.fia.com/news/briton-joyner-wins-kf-world-championship-end|date=13 February 2014|website=FIA|title=The Briton Joyner wins the KF World Championship in the end|access-date=21 September 2024|archive-date=20 September 2024|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240920235632/https://www.fia.com/news/briton-joyner-wins-kf-world-championship-end|url-status=live}}</ref> This was the only championship he failed to win in 2013 whilst completing all rounds. Verstappen won the South Garda Winter Cup in [[KF2]],<ref>{{cite web|title=South Garda Winter Cup – KF2 2013|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/south-garda-winter-cup---kf2/2013|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=9 October 2023|archive-date=10 October 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231010192604/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/south-garda-winter-cup---kf2/2013|url-status=live}}</ref> as well as the [[WSK Euro Series]] and the [[WSK Super Master Series]] in [[KZ1 (karting)|KZ1]] and [[KZ2]], respectively.<ref>{{cite web|title=WSK Euro Series – KZ1 2013|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/wsk-euro-series---kz1/2013|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=9 October 2023|archive-date=10 October 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231010192559/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/wsk-euro-series---kz1/2013|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{cite web|title=WSK Master Series – KZ2 2013|url=https://www.driverdb.com/championships/wsk-master-series---kz2/2013|work=DriverDB.com|access-date=9 October 2023|archive-date=8 October 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231008013540/https://www.driverdb.com/championships/wsk-master-series---kz2/2013|url-status=live}}</ref>

=== Lower formulae (2014) ===
{{Multiple images
| perrow            = 2
| total_width       = 350
| image1            = 2014 F3 HockenheimringII Max Verstappen by 2eight DSC6547.jpg
| image2            = 2014 F3 HockenheimringII Max Verstappen by 2eight DSC7625 (cropped less).jpg
| footer            = Verstappen competed in the [[FIA Formula 3 European Championship]] in [[2014 FIA Formula 3 European Championship|2014]], after joining the [[Red Bull Junior Team]].
